\section{Methodology}\label{methodology}

This gazetteer compiles 198 excavated Migration Period (c.~250-750AD)
and Early Medieval transitional settlements, promontory hillforts
(\emph{gorodishcha}), riverine villages (\emph{selishcha}), and
fortified towns across the six macro-regions of Belarus, Ukraine, and
Russia (excluding the Baltic provinces). All records are stored in the
companion settlement JSON file in the standard Stadester dictionary
format, keyed by settlement and country identifier with English and
Russian names, decimal coordinates, and positive integer population
estimates keyed by calendar year.

For each settlement, population figures during the Migration Period
(250-750AD) and the Early Medieval transition (900AD) are derived from
excavated dwelling counts, fortified and unfortified habitation areas,
and associated cemetery burial totals
\citeproc{ref-oblomskiy2016kolochinskaya}{{[}1{]}}\citeproc{ref-krenke2019moskvorechye}{{[}2{]}}\citeproc{ref-goldina1999drevnyaya}{{[}3{]}}\citeproc{ref-gadzhiev2002derbent}{{[}4{]}}.
In the JSON file, only inhabited benchmark years are recorded so that
the first and last keys represent each site's documented occupation
span; in the tables below, benchmarks prior to foundation or after
abandonment are denoted by a hyphen (-). Where a Migration Period
settlement evolved directly into a modern town or village on the same
site, its population at the First All-Russian Census of 1897 (1900AD
benchmark) is included \citeproc{ref-troinitsky1905perepis}{{[}5{]}}.

\section{Results}\label{results}

\subsection{European Forest-Steppe
Settlements}\label{european-forest-steppe-settlements}

\crdxref{Table}{tab:reg1-sites} lists 38 excavated settlements across
the Seim, Psel, Upper Don, Oskol, and Desna forest-steppe valleys,
covering the Kiev, Eastern Chernyakhov, Kolochin, Penkovka,
Volyntsevo-Romny, Borshevo, and Saltovo-Mayaki cultures
\citeproc{ref-oblomskiy2016kolochinskaya}{{[}1{]}}\citeproc{ref-shchukin2005gotskiy}{{[}6{]}}\citeproc{ref-oblomskiy2002dneprovskoe}{{[}7{]}}\citeproc{ref-sedov1999drevnerusskaya}{{[}8{]}}.

\vspace{4pt}\par

\subsection{Mixed and Boreal Forest Zone
Settlements}\label{mixed-and-boreal-forest-zone-settlements}

\crdxref{Table}{tab:reg2-sites} lists 55 excavated hillforts, riverine
villages, and proto-urban centres across the Moskva, Upper and Middle
Oka, Upper Volga, Ilmen-Volkhov, Pskov, and Upper Dnieper forest basins,
spanning the Late Dyakovo, Moshchiny, Ryazan-Oka, Tushemlya, Pskov Long
Barrows, Merya, Muroma, and Early Rus' traditions
\citeproc{ref-krenke2011dyakovo}{{[}9{]}}\citeproc{ref-mayorov2016moschiny}{{[}10{]}}\citeproc{ref-akhmedov2018ryazan}{{[}11{]}}\citeproc{ref-lopatin2007severnye}{{[}12{]}}\citeproc{ref-islanova2012ozero}{{[}13{]}}.

\subsection{Middle Volga, Kama-Vyatka, and Cis-Ural
Settlements}\label{middle-volga-kama-vyatka-and-cis-ural-settlements}

\crdxref{Table}{tab:reg3-sites} lists 44 excavated hillforts and village
complexes across the Samara Bend, Middle Volga, Kama, Vyatka, Belaya,
and Sylva basins, representing the Imenkovo, Azelino, Mazunino, Turaevo,
Bakhmutino, Turbasly, Kushnarenkovo, Nevolino, Lomovatovo, and Polom
cultures
\citeproc{ref-goldina1999drevnyaya}{{[}3{]}}\citeproc{ref-vyazov2019middle}{{[}14{]}}\citeproc{ref-goldina2012nevolino}{{[}15{]}}\citeproc{ref-bakhshiev2017bakhmutino}{{[}16{]}}\citeproc{ref-nazarov2025lomovatovo}{{[}17{]}}.

\subsection{Open Pontic-Caspian Steppe
Settlements}\label{open-pontic-caspian-steppe-settlements}

\crdxref{Table}{tab:reg4-sites} lists 16 excavated emporia, seasonal
winter camps, river-delta towns, and Khazar brick and stone fortresses
across the Lower Don, Lower Volga, and Southern Trans-Ural steppe
\citeproc{ref-shchukin2005gotskiy}{{[}6{]}}\citeproc{ref-botalov2009gunny}{{[}18{]}}\citeproc{ref-kazanski2020steppe}{{[}19{]}}.

\subsection{North Caucasus and Caspian Corridor
Settlements}\label{north-caucasus-and-caspian-corridor-settlements}

\crdxref{Table}{tab:reg5-sites} lists 22 excavated fortress cities,
terraced mountain strongholds, and coastal settlements across Dagestan,
the Central Caucasus (Alanian Kislovodsk and Kuban valleys), and the
Taman-Black Sea littoral
\citeproc{ref-gadzhiev2002derbent}{{[}4{]}}\citeproc{ref-gmyrya2022palasa}{{[}20{]}}\citeproc{ref-korobov2013terraced}{{[}21{]}}\citeproc{ref-mastykova2009kuban}{{[}22{]}}.

\vspace{4pt}\par

\subsection{Western and Southern Siberian
Settlements}\label{western-and-southern-siberian-settlements}

\crdxref{Table}{tab:reg6-sites} lists 23 excavated fortified
settlements, iron-working villages, and agro-pastoral centres across the
Tobol-Irtysh, Middle and Lower Ob, Minusinsk Basin, Russian Altai, and
Lake Baikal regions, covering the Sargat, Potchevash, Relka, Tashtyk,
Bulan-Koby, Early Turkic, and Kurykan traditions
\citeproc{ref-matveeva2000sargat}{{[}23{]}}\citeproc{ref-zelenkov2025potchevash}{{[}24{]}}\citeproc{ref-azbelev2007oglakhty}{{[}25{]}}\citeproc{ref-seregin2021altai}{{[}26{]}}\citeproc{ref-kharinsky2005baikal}{{[}27{]}}.
